## Good idea → Quick task, and Discuss on ideas (branch `feat/idea-quick-task-dispatcher`) — 2026-11-09

- Good idea opens Quick task (QUICK-TASK-SPEC §10) when *Quick task for New task* is on, else the full form as before: a project idea, an idea whose card is finished, archived or gone (no menu any more; its reason line moved into Other options…), the menu's Start a new task and an idea link. The dialog is locked to the idea's project (`quickProjects`), its text is the idea's title and detail (`ideaQuickText`), and the card gets the idea's title (no AI title), the full form's `ideaDescription`, the idea's type, priority and tags, and `ideaId` (`quickCreateInput`; the daemon's existing `tasks.create` idea path accepts it). More options… hands all of it to the full form (`quickMoreSeed`). **Other options…** lists Open the full form and, for a card that can still continue, the menu's Continue / Send to another session… / Do it in a new session, in place in the dialog (the board's menus cannot show over a Modal). The menu's order is unchanged; its logic moved into `client/idea-choice.ts` (`goodIdeaPlan`, `ideaOtherOptions`, `newTaskFromIdea`).
- Discuss (DISPATCHER-SPEC §9.5): open ideas get **Discuss** before Dismiss in the drawer and on the Ideas page while the board can open the dispatcher chat (`discussAvailable`, the toolbar's rule). It opens the chat (desktop un-minimizes; a phone closes an open card over the chat page) and puts `discussIdeaText` in the message box through the panel's `fill` prop, an outside draft change (the native input remounts with it). It never sends and never replaces a draft: the text goes after what is typed, once (`discussDraft`, on `nextMoveDraft`).
  - Checks: the new cases in `client/idea-choice.test.ts` and `client/quick-task.test.ts` failed first (both files failed to import the missing exports), the three Discuss cases in `client/dispatcher-panel-logic.test.ts` and the new tip case in `client/onboarding-logic.test.ts` failed first; then `npm run typecheck` exit 0 and `npm test` (counts in the delivery report). Unrun: the dialog, the Discuss button and the chat fill in a live client (desktop or phone).
  - Onboarding: new one-time tip `idea-discuss` (after `first-idea`; pressing Discuss marks it seen) and a README sentence. No tour change (the tour's Good idea demo still turns the idea into a task). Telemetry: no heartbeat field (an entry point to existing actions). Strings: five new in all 9 catalogs; "Start a new task · Suggested" removed (unused).
- Good idea on a `scope=new` (or no scope) idea of a live card opens Quick task directly when *Quick task for New task* is on; the card's actions are in its Other options… (`goodIdeaPlan` takes `quick`; the board passes the setting to the drawer's `TaskIdeas` and the Ideas page). With it off, or for `scope=here`, the menu is unchanged (QUICK-TASK-SPEC §10, IDEAS-SPEC §6). IDEAS-INBOX-SPEC §2.3 needed no change. No new strings, tip or telemetry change.

## Dispatcher idea tools (branch `feat/idea-quick-task-dispatcher`) — 2026-11-09

- The dispatcher reads and keeps a project's ideas (DISPATCHER-SPEC §5.1, PROJECT-IDEAS-SPEC §4): `flowly_list_ideas` (a read; card and project ideas together; `status` open by default, `accepted`, `dismissed` or `all`; `tag`, `type`, `query` over title and detail, `limit` 1–200, default 50; newest first through the Ideas page's own order, now `ideaNewestFirst` in `shared/ideas.ts`); `flowly_update_idea` (a write; the card sessions' edit code, `changeIdea` split out of `manageIdea`; full `tags` or `addTags`/`removeTags`; card ideas' changes are events on their card, project ideas' none; `IDEAS_DISABLED` while Ideas are off); `flowly_create_card` takes `ideaId` and accepts the idea through the New card path, refusing another project's or a closed idea before anything is made. Both names are in `dispatcherToolNames`, so a dispatcher saved with the first eight (or `savedTools: true`) shows the new-tools warning; `firstSavedTools` is unchanged.
  - Checks: the new cases in `server/dispatcher-tools.test.ts` and the changed one in `server/dispatcher.test.ts` failed first (5 failed); then `npm run typecheck` exit 0 and `npm test` (counts in the delivery report).
  - Onboarding and telemetry: no change (agent-facing tools; no UI, switch or heartbeat field). Strings: none (agent-facing text stays English).
- Review fixes: a replayed `idempotencyKey` whose `ideaId` differs from the first call's (or is added or dropped) is `REQUEST_REUSED` and leaves the idea open; losing a race for an idea (two creates at once, or a dismissal between check and write) is `IDEA_CLOSED`, not `CARD_CREATE_FAILED`; empty `addTags`/`removeTags` alone are `INVALID_INPUT`. Ruling (Noah 2026-11-09): ideas of Done, Cancelled and archived cards can be edited and dismissed (DISPATCHER-SPEC §5.1). Tests added for a card idea's `origin.taskId` and for the dispatcher's own idea events staying out of its change line (both held already).
  - Checks: the new cases in `server/dispatcher-tools.test.ts` failed first (3 failed: replay, race, empty tag lists; the ruling, origin and change-line cases passed as written); then `npm run typecheck` and `npm test` (counts in the delivery report).

## Single pane final fix round (branch `feat/single-pane`) — 2026-11-09

- Dispatcher lifecycle (rulings 2026-11-08, "one long-lived session"): the `context_full` state, its panel text (removed from all 9 catalogs) and the start-fresh path are gone. `isDeadDispatcher` is the one test for a dead session (archived, closed or missing; `error` counts as alive, ruling 2026-11-09). A start is refused unless the stored session is dead; before replacing it Flowly reads it again and archives it only if it is still dead and not archived yet (that id only; a failed archive is logged and the start goes on); the old id joins `previousAgentIds` in `dispatcher.json`. Notes are never dropped while the dispatcher lives; notes in the start window are held until Paseo has created the session; after a turn end, held notes are re-checked at 2/5/15/30/60 s and then every 60 s until they go out, and a `dispatcher.status` read that sees idle delivers them. Note-drop counts are logged with a fixed label and the count as context. DISPATCHER-SPEC §1–§3, §7 (the remaining idle-read → send race stated), §9.3, §10; AGENTS.md dispatcher exception (dead-session archive).
  - Checks: the new `server/dispatcher.test.ts` cases failed first (9 failed: missing `isDeadDispatcher`/`RECHECK_MAX_MS`, no archive, no start-window hold, no backoff, notes dropped on a full context); then `npm run typecheck` exit 0, `npm test` exit 0 (172 files, 1609 passed, 1 skipped).
  - Onboarding and telemetry: no change (recovery behavior only; no new switch or core action, no heartbeat field).
- Dispatcher limits and gates (rulings 2026-11-08): the dispatcher's creations and starts count in their own bucket, 40 per hour (`agentCardLimits.dispatcherPerHour`), apart from the card agents' 20; the Agents create and edit cards switch gates every dispatcher write, `flowly_link_cards` and `flowly_propose_idea` included (ideas keep the Ideas switch too); a project with a missing root can be read, its writes get `ROOT_UNAVAILABLE`; `LEGACY_PROJECT` is gone (legacy projects follow the card agents' rules; charter updated); the change line leaves out the actions of every dispatcher session of this install (`previousAgentIds`). Interpretation: the 40/hour counts the same things as the card agents' limit (creations and starts), not edits, links or ideas. DISPATCHER-SPEC §4, §5, §9.2, §10; START-CARD-SPEC §3.
  - Checks: the new and changed cases in `server/dispatcher-tools.test.ts` and `server/dispatcher-items.test.ts` failed first (5 failed); then `npm run typecheck` exit 0 and `npm test` exit 0 (counts in the final-fix report).
  - Onboarding and telemetry: no change (no new switch; an existing switch's reach widened).
- Delivery mode editable until delivery (ruling 2026-11-08): Edit shows the Delivery choice, seeded from the card's effective mode and sent only when the user changed it; read-only (options disabled) while the card is In review, Committed or Integrated (`deliveryModeLocked`), and `taskEdit.deliveryMode` with a different value is refused by the daemon then. The stored `Task.deliveryMode` has no length cap any more. DELIVERY-MODE-SPEC §2, §5, §6; SPEC-OUTLINE line. Not done (optional): aligning `deliveryLine` wording with the skill text.
  - Checks: the new cases in `shared/delivery-mode.test.ts` and `server/store.test.ts` failed first (3 failed); then `npm run typecheck` exit 0 and `npm test` exit 0 (counts in the final-fix report). Unrun: the form in a live client (desktop or phone).
  - Strings: none new (the existing "Delivery" title and mode labels). Onboarding: no tip or tour change (a small extension of an existing choice; the tour does not describe it). Telemetry: no field.
- `flowly_start_card` hardening: a refused move (the card changed during the create, or another card is bound to the worktree) archives the unused worktree as the meanwhile case does, unless a card refers to it or it has a session; after the create the worktree is read, and one that is archived or gone (the same key gave back a worktree a refusal archived) refuses with `START_FAILED` and asks for a new key. The worktree request (tracking branch, kept request, root Git check) is made after the hourly limit and the model check, so `CARD_LIMIT` and `MODEL_UNAVAILABLE` create nothing. An unreadable kept request is logged, left on disk and refuses with `START_FAILED`. Starts queue per project root instead of one global queue; root lookups run in call order so two starts of one card keep their order. START-CARD-SPEC §3, §4.1, §4.2 (the session re-check → board write window stated), §9; AGENTS.md start-card exception.
  - Checks: the new and changed cases in `server/start-card.test.ts` failed first (4 failed: no archive on a refused move, branch made before `MODEL_UNAVAILABLE`, a corrupt kept request gave `INVALID_INPUT`, another project's start waited); then `npm run typecheck` exit 0 and `npm test` exit 0 (counts in the final-fix report). A first per-root queue that looked the root up with an unordered board read let two concurrent starts of one card swap order (flaky `two starts of one card at once`, 2 of 5 runs); serializing the lookups fixed it (8 of 8 runs passed).
  - Onboarding and telemetry: no change (failure handling only).
- Carried small items: (1) artifact extra folders are kept apart as `resolved` roots, resolved once at the settings read and compared as they are (a folder swapped for a link afterwards does not widen them); `resolveExtraRoots` does no realpath when no folder is set; `missingArtifacts` resolves the card's roots once per list. CARD-ARTIFACTS-SPEC (2026-11-08 increment). (2) Export: a note of only unbalanced parentheses (such as `(`) now counts as no note, so a title ending in an added-style parenthetical keeps it on re-import; a balanced `()` is kept as `added <date>, ()`. IDEAS-IMPORT-EXPORT-SPEC §7. (3) Start again's `NEEDS_WORKTREE` text names both causes ("…already had sessions, or Flowly didn't create it for the card…"), in all 9 catalogs; DISPATCHER-SPEC §9.4.
  - Checks: the new cases in `server/artifacts.test.ts`, `shared/board-export.test.ts` and `client/answered-asks.test.ts` failed first (1 each); then `npm run typecheck` exit 0 and `npm test` exit 0 (counts in the final-fix report). Unrun: the `^CODE:` parse of a refusal from a live host.
  - Onboarding and telemetry: no change.
- Live script hardening (`docs/verification/single-pane-live.mjs`): sessions it starts carry `single-pane-live-run=<runId>` and only those (or a marked card's, or its own dispatcher) are archived; a dispatcher created before the run's `startedAt` is never archived; only the fixture projects' temp directories are removed; the signal notice mentions `--yes-archive-dispatcher`; the `context_full` state is gone from the script, its test and `docs/verification/single-pane.md`. `scripts/single-pane-live.test.ts` extended (4 new cases failed first). Unrun: the script against a live daemon.
- Re-review follow-ups: (1) a batch held while the dispatcher reads running with no turn end since (a turn end with nothing held started no chase) is re-checked every 60 s; (2) a start holds notes from before it takes the board summary until it ends (success or refusal), so a note that arrives during a replacement goes to the new dispatcher instead of being dropped for the dead one; notes held when the start began are covered by the summary, later ones are kept (one may repeat it). DISPATCHER-SPEC §7, §10. (3) AGENTS.md dispatcher exception: unavailable roots are readable with writes refused; the switch gates its writes; its own 40/hour limit. (4) Live script: a dispatcher whose read fails at cleanup is left in place. (5) Live script: a fixture directory carries a git-excluded run marker file written before it is recorded, so cleanup removes one this run made but never registered; others stay. (6) The per-root queue test uses a gate instead of a 300 ms race.
  - Checks: the new cases in `server/dispatcher.test.ts` (2) and `scripts/single-pane-live.test.ts` (2) failed first; the rewritten queue test passed 5 of 5 runs (not re-run against a global queue: it would hang to the test timeout there by construction). Then `npm run typecheck` exit 0 and `npm test` exit 0 (counts in the final-fix report).
- Rulings recorded (Noah, 2026-11-09): a dispatcher in `error` counts as alive and is never replaced automatically (DISPATCHER-SPEC §3, no code change); the idle-read → send race of DISPATCHER-SPEC §7 is accepted.
- Delivery mode locks for good (ruling 2026-11-09): the store sets `Task.deliveredAt` in the write that first puts a card in In review, Committed or Integrated (any writer: Edit, a drag, a status rule, an agent tool) and never clears it (a board restore from a backup taken before delivery rolls the mark back); `deliveryModeLocked(task)` is true with the mark or in one of those lanes (a card stored before the mark, no migration). The daemon refuses a different mode and Edit shows it read-only after a move back too. DELIVERY-MODE-SPEC §2, §6.
  - Checks: the new and changed cases in `shared/delivery-mode.test.ts` (1) and `server/store.test.ts` (2) failed first; then `npm run typecheck` exit 0 and `npm test` exit 0 (counts in the final-fix report). Unrun: the form in a live client.
  - Strings: none new (the refusal is a daemon error, English). Onboarding and telemetry: no change (a stricter rule on an existing choice).
- Dispatcher folder out of the plugin data folder (security ruling 2026-11-09): the dispatcher works in `<Paseo home>/flowly-dispatcher/dispatcher` (`dispatcherFolder`, from the same Paseo home as the data folder), whose parent holds only it, so providers that auto-allow reads and edits inside the cwd no longer cover the board, sealing key, database or settings (not a sandbox: full-access modes and absolute paths still reach any user file; the boundary is the sealed credential and the permission prompts). Every start, before reuse or creation, refuses (`START_FAILED`, logged) a folder inside, holding, or next to the data folder: as given, on the real path of its existing ancestors before anything is made (a linked `flowly-dispatcher` creates nothing; a dangling link is refused), and on its real path once it exists (a folder replaced by a link is refused, reuse included); the folder and its parent are set to 0700 at each start. The stored workspace is reused only while its directory is the dispatcher folder, so one in the old `<dataDir>/dispatcher` makes the next start create a new workspace (refused while the old session is alive; a dead one archived as before); the old folder and workspace are never moved, deleted or archived. `ownsRoot` recognizes both folders (Add project, restore and resources hide or refuse both), and New task with an existing workspace also refuses one whose directory is either folder (the old workspace is no longer the stored one). DISPATCHER-SPEC §2, §10; AGENTS.md dispatcher exception; SPEC-OUTLINE line. Security re-review fixes (I1, M1–M5, I2): the real-path check now runs before reuse and before `mkdir`, existing folders are chmod 0700, `tool-server.test.ts` passes a temp dispatcher folder, the wording no longer claims a sandbox, and the old folder's Paseo project stays registered (hidden).
  - Checks: the new and changed cases in `server/dispatcher.test.ts` failed first (`dispatcherFolder` missing, then the old-workspace bind refused with the wrong error); then `npm run typecheck` exit 0 and `npm test` exit 0 (counts in the final-fix report). Unrun: a start against a live daemon.
  - Strings: none new (daemon errors stay English). Onboarding and telemetry: no change (no user-visible behavior beyond where the folder is).
- Dispatcher start failed on the installed build ("MCP server 'flowly-dispatcher' from plugin flowly collides with agent saved configuration"): Paseo's daemon refuses any MCP server a plugin's open hook adds under a name the agent's saved config already has, and the dispatcher was created with `flowly-dispatcher` saved while the open hook also added it at the first open (the record names the new id before create). The create no longer saves it; the hook is the one source, at every open. Card starts are not affected: they save `flowly` and the hook never adds that name (it adds `flowly-board` only to sessions outside cards). DISPATCHER-SPEC §3.
  - Checks: the fake Paseo in `server/dispatcher-tools.test.ts` now applies the daemon's rule with what `dispatcherSession` (the hook's source) adds; the new case and an existing replacement case failed with the same message first. Then `npm run typecheck` exit 0 and `npm test` exit 0 (counts in the final-fix report). Unrun: a dispatcher start on the installed build after the fix.
- Dispatcher sat on a hidden permission prompt (live, agent in Always Ask): its own `flowly_create_card` call asked for permission, and the dispatcher chat shows no permission requests. Its own tools no longer prompt: the session is created with `flowly-dispatcher` saved and a `toolPolicy` that preapproves each of its eight tools by exact name (`dispatcherToolNames` in `shared/dispatcher.ts`), Paseo's supported preapproval; Paseo requires the grant's server in the same create request, so the server is saved again and the open hook no longer adds it to such a dispatcher (the record notes `savedTools: true`; one stored without it, started before this change, still gets the server from the hook, unapproved). A provider Paseo cannot preapprove for (anything but Claude, Codex, OpenCode) refuses the start with Paseo's message and the earlier record, `savedTools` included, is restored. Other permissions are not preapproved; showing them in the dispatcher chat with allow/deny comes with the chat rebuilt on the card session view (next entry). DISPATCHER-SPEC §1, §3, §10.
  - Checks: the fake Paseo in `server/dispatcher-tools.test.ts` also applies the daemon's `validateToolPolicyServers` rule; the new case (saved server, policy equal to the credential's `tools/list`, nothing from the hook) failed first on the hook still adding the server. A refused create restoring the earlier mark is covered in `server/dispatcher.test.ts` (checked by mutation). Then `npm run typecheck` exit 0 and `npm test` exit 0 (counts in the final-fix report). Unrun: a live dispatcher calling `flowly_create_card` in Always Ask after the reload.
  - Strings: none. Onboarding and telemetry: no change (no new capability; fewer prompts).

- Ruling on re-review finding 1 (Noah 2026-11-09): an unguarded dispatcher (no `guard`, or another version or provider) gets no board notes: `flush` keeps them held with no send and no re-check, and a start's board summary covers them. The unguarded notice now says it gets no notes until replaced and offers **Replace dispatcher** (confirm dialog): `dispatcher.replace { agentId }`, a plugin RPC only (the dispatcher's MCP server has no replace or archive tool, asserted). It refuses a guarded dispatcher (`DISPATCHER_GUARDED`) or another id (`DISPATCHER_CHANGED`), archives exactly that session (`REPLACE_FAILED` and no start when the archive fails), then starts a guarded one through Start's path. Start still refuses a live or closed one. AGENTS.md invariant amended. Tests red first (3 failed). Probes (CLI and Paseo's SDK, killed at init, `docs/verification/dispatcher-tool-allowlist.md`): strict create and resume list the 8 Flowly tools and chat_polish's 2 and only the passed servers; the non-strict baseline also loads the datadog plugin server and 37 claude.ai servers. Strings: none. Onboarding, telemetry: none.
- Review (Medium): Replace archived the old session before the start's steps that can fail, so a failed start left no dispatcher. `run` now takes the session Replace archives and archives it after the folder, workspace, MCP server, board summary and record (a failed archive restores the record); only the create follows. A create that fails after it leaves the dispatcher ended with an error saying the old one was archived; the panel refreshes its status so Start shows with it, and the confirm dialog says so in one line. Tests red first (2 failed). Strings: 1 new, all catalogs.
- Review (Low): the panel showed a permission's text box when it had no options, but the server takes text only when it has no request, so typed text could be thrown away. Chat ask rows now carry `requestId`, and `answersWithText` shows the box for a permission only when it has none. Tests red first (2 failed). Strings: none.
- Review (Low): the text answer to a permission with no request now requires the session to be `closed`, as §9.4 says; a running or idle one is refused with "Open the card to answer this request." and nothing is sent or stored. Test red first (1 failed). Strings: none (daemon error).
- Review: spec and test for e8498c6's rule: a session restored from the archive is alive again, so its asks stored as `session_ended` come back open, counted and answerable (§3, §9.4). The code already did this; the test passed on first run (no red step). Strings: none.
- Review: missing tests added: an unguarded dispatcher's turn end sets no re-check timer (fake timers, running and idle); the first send after Replace leaves out the notes its board summary covers and keeps one that arrived while it was written; Replace and Start share one queue (Replace then Start, Start then Replace, and Start on an archived one then Replace: one dispatcher made each time). All passed first run; each fails against a deliberate break of the rule it covers (unguarded check limited to idle, covered notes kept, Replace outside the queue).
- Review (Low): check 7 now selects as the bell does: `attentionItemsWithFacts` over `board.overview-changed` with the overview cards' session facts (client/board.tsx), not `attentionItems` over `board.read`. For a check card, or any card, the two give the same signals: `attentionItems` is the same function with facts taken from the board's events, and the overview's facts come from those events by the same rules (open question and blocker reports, silent turn, latest turn); the overview keeps only the latest turn's `action` and `at`, the only fields the rule reads, and gives no facts for a session with no events on the card, which the rule reads like the empty facts `attentionItems` builds. Only the source differs (the overview RPC instead of the full board). Test red first (1 failed: the fake's `board.read` now throws). Run natively with a fake context: `["q:e1","p:s1:r0"]`, exit 0. Not run live.
- Ruling (Noah 2026-11-09): an unguarded dispatcher takes no messages from the user until Replace. New `dispatcher.send` RPC (the chat's message box, which sent through the client SDK before, so the daemon could not refuse it) and `dispatcher.what-next` share `reachable`: `DISPATCHER_DOWN`, else `DISPATCHER_UNGUARDED`, nothing sent, nothing marked seen. The panel disables the box and What should I do? and shows "Replace the dispatcher to keep chatting" beside Replace; a refused send shows that text (`dispatcherStartMessage`). The composer queue cannot hold a dispatcher message (its push and drain need a card bound to the session's workspace), so nothing changed there. Not gated: answering the dispatcher's own permission requests, and messages typed in Paseo's own session view. Test red first (1 failed). Strings: 1 new, all catalogs. Onboarding, telemetry: none.
- Review: Replace stored the new session before it archived the old one, so a crash between the two left a live unguarded dispatcher no record named. The archive now comes after the MCP server and summary and before the save; a failed archive changes nothing in the record and keeps the held notes; a failed save after it leaves the old session archived and named, so the dispatcher reads ended and Start works. Test red first (1 failed: the save ran before the archive, so Replace succeeded).
- Review: spec §3 corrected to the code: while the dispatcher is unguarded the whole chat is off, its own permission requests included (`requestsDisabled` follows `canWrite`, which includes the lock), until Replace. It also names the routes Flowly cannot block: Paseo's own session view, and card sessions' `send_agent_prompt`. Docs only.

- Safety: every id recorded in `$TMPDIR/single-pane-live-<run>.json` before use; only recorded, prefixed cards inside Tinylink or the fixture project are deleted; Tinylink itself and unrecorded cards or sessions are never touched; messages go only to sessions the run started (the real dispatcher only with `--use-existing-dispatcher`); cleanup in `finally`, verified against the board and Paseo; `--cleanup <record>` finishes a crashed run.
- Written against Noah's rulings: no `context_full` check (a second `dispatcher.start` is refused while one runs; a new one replaces only a dead one). The one assertion that depends on the final fix round (the dead dispatcher is archived when a new one starts) is marked "needs the final fix round" in `--help` and `single-pane.md`. Delivery-mode editing has no check in 1–7.

- Fix round 1: `--cleanup` now validates its record file before connecting (run id, marker, file name, fixture dirs strictly inside `tmpdir()` with the fixture prefix, card titles with the marker) and refuses the whole cleanup otherwise; sessions are archived only when verified by label; Tinylink's id and root are re-resolved, never read from the record; branches are deleted only as `kanban/single-pane-live-<run>-*` with no live worktree (after `git worktree prune`); SIGINT/SIGTERM run cleanup once and exit 130/143, and the retry command prints at the start; a dispatcher the user already has (any state) makes checks 1, 3, 4 skip without `--use-existing-dispatcher`; check 1 says when its archive assertion was not exercised; check 6 skips when memory is off. `scripts/single-pane-live.test.ts` covers each guard (23 tests). `single-pane.md` documents the `touch` pre-allow timeout in check 2, concurrent-activity noise in check 7 and the memory requirement.
- Fix round 2: cleanup decides cards from the live board (marker in the current title, in Tinylink or a verified fixture project), so a forged record cannot get a real card's session archived; a memory entry is deleted only in a verified fixture project with the marker in its title; `--cleanup` lists a recorded dispatcher as "left in place" unless `--yes-archive-dispatcher`; the cleanup report prints once, a signal exit repeats the retry command and says a long-RPC check may have created more; `decideDispatcher` follows the daemon's states (`context_full` is messaged, not replaced); check 6 skips when `memory.read` reports memory off or throws. 39 tests in `scripts/single-pane-live.test.ts`, including cleanup runs against a fake daemon.
- Check 7 fix (Noah's live run at 3122df6, 2026-11-09: "after dismissing in the bell" timed out at bell 3 = chat 3, expected 2): the check dismissed the card with `attention.dismiss { signals: [second ask] }`, which replaces the card's set (ATTENTION-DISMISS-SPEC §7a), so the first ask, dismissed in the chat a step earlier and still pending, left the set and reopened: one down, one up. The product matches the spec; the check now sends all the card's live signals, as the bell does (`item.signals`), waits for both asks to read dismissed and expects the count to drop by the card's open asks. `checkBell` is exported and run against a fake daemon that keeps the two dismiss rules (`scripts/single-pane-live.test.ts`; it failed first with the live run's timeout).

The live script's help and `single-pane.md` say to set Tinylink up that way. Cleanup now also verifies the dispatcher chat and the bell: no ask or answer of any card the record names is left (`dispatcher.items-changed`; the bell counts exactly the chat's open asks). Not run live.

Session-ended rows show Dismiss (stored as dismissed in the chat).

The panel refreshes it after an answer or dismissal.

## Dispatcher agent, charter and cross-project scope (branch `feat/single-pane`, Task 9) — 2026-11-08

- `server/dispatcher.ts`: `Dispatcher` (record `<dataDir>/dispatcher.json`, atomic, corrupt file kept as `.invalid-<ms>`; workspace `<dataDir>/dispatcher/` created with `background: true` and no Paseo project id, reused, never archived; `status`, `start`, `deliver`, `turnEnded`, `owns`), `dispatcherState`, `dispatcherSummary`, `noteMessage`, `wireDispatcher` (bus → `facts.filter(isStop)` → batcher → deliver; returns the unload cleanup). `server/dispatcher-tools.ts`: the eight dispatcher tools and the caller check (`DISPATCHER_REPLACED`, `SESSION_MISMATCH`, then `NOT_FOUND` / `PROJECT_ARCHIVED` / `ROOT_UNAVAILABLE` via `AgentCards.dispatcherCaller`). `server/tool-context.ts`: sealed purpose `dispatcher` over `{ agentId, workspaceId }`; `authenticateAny` returns `kind: "dispatcher"`. `server/tool-server.ts`: a dispatcher credential's MCP server registers only the dispatcher tools; `/api` still refuses non-card credentials; `dispatcherSession` for `agent.session_open`. `server/background-workspace.ts`: `createDirectoryWorkspace` (shared; maps the host's "Update the host to use background workspaces." to `HOST_UNSUPPORTED`, no retry). `server/service.ts`: `reservedWorkspace` refuses binding the dispatcher workspace to a card (New task "existing", PR review cards), so no archive/wrap-up/switch path can reach it. `shared/dispatcher.ts`: status/start schemas, `dispatcherCharter`, `dispatcherContextLimit`. `shared/rpc.ts`: `dispatcher.status`, `dispatcher.start`. `index.server.ts`: RPCs, `agent.turn_ended` → `turnEnded`, session_open adds `flowly-dispatcher` only for the stored agent id, unload unwires (batcher disposed, subscription removed). The MCP result helper moved to `toolResult` in `server/tool-error.ts` (shared by both servers).
- Task 8 review items: `NoteBatcher.dispose()`; `firstAt` sentinel `null` (with 0 a batch whose first fact read time 0 flushed at 65 s instead of 60 s); `commitHandler` builds no facts without subscribers (`EventBus.hasSubscribers`); the dispatcher subscriber pushes only stops. Each has a test.

Links made by the dispatcher are stored with `createdBy: "system"` (the link schema has no agent value) and logged as `linked` events with `actor: "agent"` and its agent id.

## Start a card from a tool (branch `feat/single-pane`, Task 5) — 2026-11-08

- `flowly_start_card` (`server/start-card.ts`, `CardStarter`) on the card and board MCP servers: checks setting, project (`NOT_FOUND`/`PROJECT_ARCHIVED`), card (`NOT_FOUND`/`CARD_FINISHED`), replays a session this `operationId` already prepared (`ToolSecrets.preparedFor`, no new resources), refuses a card with a live session (`CARD_HAS_SESSION`), then per Q2(b): worktree → start there; linked-root directory → start there; directory workspace with `ownsWorkspace`, no `previousWorkspaces`/`switch` and no session ever → new worktree (`flowly-start:<operationId>` key, request kept on disk per operation for retries), card moved in one write (`previousWorkspaces`, `workspace_switched` by the agent), the empty directory workspace re-checked and archived; anything else `NEEDS_WORKTREE`. Cards older than `ownsWorkspace` count as not owned.

Model per Q6: project default, else global (Settings → Models, wired in `index.server.ts`), else the project's latest session (`startingAgent`/`lastSessionSettings`); mode falls back to `suggestedMode`; reasoning to the provider default; speed not set (`MODEL_UNAVAILABLE` when there is nothing).

An existing local branch is checked out (`action: "checkout"`) when no worktree has it checked out (`git worktree list`), else the generated name.
- `flowly_create_card` gains `workspace: "directory" | "worktree"` (`WORKTREE_UNAVAILABLE` for linked-root projects); creations and starts share the 20-per-hour counter (`AgentCards.checkLimit`/`countCreation`). It has no Linear input, so its worktrees get the generated name.

Its session is hard-deleted like the memory review's (AGENTS.md exceptions extended).

Project (ruling 2026-11-05): New task and `N` use the board's project; only `Q` and the Command Center start from the last quick-task project. ## Switch a card between local and worktree (card "switch local and worktree") — 2026-10-29

- New card action *Switch to worktree…*: Flowly snapshots the main checkout (HEAD, `git diff --binary HEAD`, untracked files), creates a worktree at that commit, copies everything in, writes a handoff folder (`.flowly/handoff/<id>/`: `handoff.md` as a user input, old sessions' transcripts, `snapshot.patch`), moves the card (`previousWorkspaces`, `switch`), and archives the old workspace and sessions. Nothing starts. *Switch to local…* goes back; its dialog shows both branches with their last 5 commits and the changes per folder (first two directory levels) side by side; with uncommitted worktree changes the user picks *Copy* (applied as unstaged changes, checked per file in a few batch runs: already there is skipped, a conflict disables it), *Overwrite* (Copy plus the worktree's version of each conflicting file, the main checkout's saved to `main-before/`) or *Discard*; either way they are first saved as `worktree-uncommitted.patch` in the handoff folder and the plugin data folder. [WORKSPACE-SWITCH-SPEC](WORKSPACE-SWITCH-SPEC.md)
- Agent tool `flowly_handover` (CLI `handover`) stores the card's own files and notes; *Ask {session} for a handover* sends a fixed prompt to an idle session. After a switch the new session trims the worktree, then the user presses *Release from main…*: Flowly removes exactly the worktree's diff from the main checkout's working tree (and index where staged), after `git apply --check` both ways; backup patch, *Undo release*.
- Agent tool `flowly_integrate` (CLI `integrate`): fast-forward only, applied as a patch to the main checkout's index and working tree, compare-and-swap `update-ref`; refuses `RELEASE_PENDING`, `REBASE_NEEDED`, `CONFLICT`. The agent then sets Integrated. Skill text and `AGENTS.md` updated (new exception; the `git reset origin/main` recipe points switched cards to `flowly_integrate`).
- History follows the card: archived sessions, usage, insights and workflow insights also read the earlier workspaces; their archived sessions open read-only.
- 58 new strings in all catalogs. Onboarding: a *Switch to a worktree* line in the tour's More features; no tip (the release notice explains the only non-obvious step). Telemetry: none.
- Checks: `server/workspace-git.test.ts` (15), `server/workspace-switch.test.ts` (12), `shared/workspace-switch.test.ts` (3), handover write in `server/agent-service.test.ts`, CLI refusals in `server/tool-server.test.ts`; `npm run typecheck` and `npm test` on HEAD plus this card's changes in a temporary worktree. Live check (§15) not run yet.

## Telemetry error fixes (card "Recent error investigation") — 2026-11-10

- `server/local-daemon.ts`: `paseo.pid` is read again (10 × 50 ms, like Paseo's `readPidLock`) while Paseo rewrites it in place; a missing or still unreadable file and a connection the daemon does not complete are `LocalDaemonUnavailable` (logged, not reported). A rejected local credential (`DaemonAuthenticationError`, found by the independent Haiku check) and a Paseo without the needed methods stay reported. [WORKSPACE-RESTORE-SPEC](WORKSPACE-RESTORE-SPEC.md) step 1, [TELEMETRY-SPEC §11.2](TELEMETRY-SPEC.md).

No new property: the `$exception` key set is unchanged. [TELEMETRY-SPEC §11.3](TELEMETRY-SPEC.md). The in-app notice is unchanged (a Paseo request name is not user content); README and OVERVIEW.md now say a failed Paseo request sends the request's name.

## Auto review reads every repository of a linked root — 2026-11-07

- Bug: in a linked root (PROJECT-PICKER-SPEC) the workspace directory is not a Git repository, so the review's `git diff`, HEAD and `ls-files` were empty, the shape was 0 and the commands ran in a folder without code (Admin+SMITH, 2026-10-30: "diff is empty, this workspace is not a git repository").
- [GUARDRAILS-SPEC](GUARDRAILS-SPEC.md) §6.2, §6.3: when the directory is not a repository, `linkedRepos` reads its non-hidden children (link or folder) that hold `.git` (at most 20). Shape (`linkedShape`) is their sum; the review diff (`linkedReviewDiff`) has one section per repository with every path under its name (`--src-prefix`/`--dst-prefix`), each repository cut to an equal share of the 64 KiB (the live dry run on Admin-SMITH showed agent-smith's own uncommitted diff filling all 64 KiB and hiding search-admin), so findings, B1 evidence (the file exists through the link) and rework messages cite `SMITH/src/x.ts`. `fingerprint.repos` (new optional field in `shared/model.ts`) holds each repository's sha; `fingerprint.sha` is a digest of them; round 2 diffs each repository since its own sha.
- Commands (`commandApplies`, `runInRepos`): each runs in the repositories that have it (package script, makefile target, `go.mod`, `Cargo.toml`; anything else everywhere), in order, until one fails; none applies → `skipped`, never Unavailable. A directory that is a repository behaves as before.
- Commands run only in the repositories this delivery changed (`changedRepos`: uncommitted or untracked changes, or a commit after the card was created; a repository git cannot read counts; none changed → all). The idea's first rule, "its paths are in the shape", could not narrow anything: without a base the shape includes each repository's last five commits (D6), so every repository with history counts. The user chose the rule above (2026-11-07). In Admin+SMITH today both repositories have uncommitted work from elsewhere, so both still run.
- Where a command ran (2026-11-07, after PR #93): in a linked root each command item's `detail` is `Ran in <repo>, <repo>.` (the repositories it ran in, a failing one last), passed or not; the review drawer shows it under the command when the row is opened (`client/task-review.tsx`), and `flowly_get_task` already passes `detail` to the agent. A directory that is a repository writes none. Live on Admin+SMITH (`make test` added temporarily, Ask SMITH rerun, then removed and Auto review turned back off): `detail` was `Ran in agent-smith.`.
- Onboarding and telemetry: none (internal correctness fix, nothing new to learn or count).
- Checks: `npm run typecheck` exit 0; `npm test` exit 0; new tests on real temporary repositories behind symlinks. Live on Admin+SMITH (Paseo 0.15.3, Auto review and two commands added temporarily, then removed): shape 282 files instead of 0, `fingerprint.repos` for both repositories, a finding citing `agent-smith/docs/…`, `make test` passed in agent-smith and `npm run check-types` in search-admin (the root has neither a Makefile nor a package.json).

## PR #75 review fixes: quote evidence, one timeout across refused permissions — 2026-11-07

- Auto review B1 (CodeRabbit, Major): a failed check whose only evidence was a quote was accepted even when the quote was invented. A quote now counts only when it appears, after collapsing runs of whitespace and ignoring case, as a substring of the card title, the card description, a message of the filtered conversation the reviewer was given, or that check's own user words (`hasEvidence` in `shared/auto-review.ts`, sources built in `server/auto-review.ts` `judge`). Otherwise the verdict becomes `na` with reason `no evidence`. File evidence (a file in the diff or an existing repository file) is unchanged and still counts alongside an invented quote. GUARDRAILS-SPEC §0.1 B1 is to be updated by its owner. Tests in `shared/auto-review.test.ts` and `server/auto-review.test.ts`.
- `finishRefusingPermissions` (`server/summary-models.ts`): the timeout restarted after each refused permission, so a run could take up to 21 × the timeout. One deadline now covers the whole run; each wait gets the remaining time, and a run past the deadline ends as `timeout`. Test in `server/summary-models.test.ts`.
- Onboarding and telemetry: none (internal correctness fixes).

Title `font.md` bold, never clamped; body `font.sm` at 1.5 line height, six lines, pressing the card unfolds/folds it (per card, while the page is open); source line; `#tag #tag · Used by n cards` in one small muted line. Right-click / long-press open the same menu.

- Checks: see the commit's delivery note. Unrun: plugin reload, screenshots, phone.

## Temporary sessions run Claude in Plan Mode — 2026-10-30

- Bug follow-up 2 (2026-10-30): the Ask SMITH card sat in Attention with nothing to do because its review ended `unavailable` ("asked for a permission") and that signal stays until the card moves. Plan Mode only helps Claude profiles; a Codex review session still stopped on its first tool call. `finishRefusingPermissions` (`server/summary-models.ts`, used by `runSummaryModels` and the workflow-insights run) now answers each pending permission request with deny and waits for the turn again, up to 20 times, so no temporary session fails on a permission anymore. Tests in `server/summary-models.test.ts` (refused and continues; unrefusable and endless still fail). Unavailable still raises Attention for real failures (spec §3.2); I left that as designed. AUTO-REVIEW-SPEC §4.4, TASK-SUMMARY-SPEC §2.
- Bug follow-up (2026-10-30): the same error came back as "claude-compass-smith-sqp/claude-sonnet-5-5: The summary model asked for a permission." `readOnlyMode` only matched the provider id `claude`, so Claude Code profiles named `claude-*` (SMITH/RCMD/KB SQP, AICodeWith) still ran in Always Ask. It now matches `claude` and `claude-*`; `codex-*` profiles stay on their default. Test added in `shared/summary.test.ts`. The match is by name, not by the provider's advertised modes (the SDK's provider list does not carry them without another call per provider).
- Bug: an auto review ended `unavailable` with "claude/claude-haiku-5-5: The summary model asked for a permission." The review session was created without a mode, so Claude ran in Always Ask and its first tool call blocked on a permission. `readOnlyMode` / `readOnlyConfig` in `shared/summary.ts` give `claude/*` the `plan` mode and leave other providers on their default (Codex has no read-only mode; never full access). Used by the review session, the card summarizer, the memory finished-card review, the Insights digest, workflow insights, the guardrails catch-up and the outside-session insights run (it did not carry the parent's mode). AUTO-REVIEW-SPEC §4.4, TASK-SUMMARY-SPEC §2.
- Onboarding: no tour update or tip is warranted; this is an internal reliability fix with nothing new for the user to learn.
- Unrun: a live Claude review after the change (needs a plugin reload).

## Guardrails rework after the live verification — 2026-10-29

Three findings of [the live verification](verification/guardrails-live-2026-10-29/REPORT.md) (items 2.3, 2.6 and the shape note):

- The change shape counted the wrong commits (GUARDRAILS-SPEC §6.2): `git diff HEAD` plus `git log -5` on a worktree card is the base branch's history, so every Tinylink card measured `m`. A worktree card now stores `task.baseRef` at creation (the ref `resolveWorktreeBaseRef` already resolved; `Task` had no such field, so cards created earlier fall back), and `reviewShape` / `reviewDiff` take its change from `git merge-base <baseRef> HEAD` (`git diff <base> --numstat`, round 1 diff `git diff <base>`). Directory, linked and older cards, or a failed merge-base, keep the old method; round ≥ 2 keeps `git diff <previous sha>`.
- Untracked files were invisible (§6.2, §6.3): a 450-line new fixture measured `m` and the reviewer never saw it. Shape and diff now add `git ls-files --others --exclude-standard -z` in every mode: one file each, text lines counted (a NUL in the first 8 KiB is binary, 0 lines), and the diff appends `git diff --no-index -- /dev/null <file>` under `# New files (untracked)` before the 64 KiB cut. The index is never touched.
- Corrections were judged too strictly (§4.2): "Adding a new requirement is not a correction" made the model classify `The new interface must also go into the README` / `The new copy must also go into all languages` (Chinese messages in the original) as none. The rule now says a message that the delivered change should also / still / should have done something that could have been part of it is a correction even when short; only unrelated work (another feature, another page) is a new requirement.
- Follow-up (same day): untracked files over 1 MiB count as one file with 0 lines and are only named in the review diff; PR #75 review threads: a failed Auto review write after Create project toasts and still closes the form (no duplicate project), the start-up migration's rejection is logged, and a failed migration now refuses guardrails writes (reads stay empty) so the next start migrates again instead of finding a `guardrails.json` that the first write had created; hook writers log that refusal with `logFailure`.

## Guardrails phase B: the delivery review and the agent surface (card 2458cff4) — 2026-10-28

- Review (GUARDRAILS-SPEC §6): `AutoReviewService` reads the project's guardrails at start (`GuardrailsService.reviewInput`): active and observing commands always, the built-in goal always, and while Guardrails is on (B4) the active guidelines in scope for the card's type and this delivery's size plus the card's open items. The delivery's shape comes from `git diff HEAD --numstat` plus the last five commits (`reviewShape`, binary 0, renames to the new path) and is stored as `review.shape`; `review.items` replaces `rules` (old records read their `rules` as items; `rules` stays optional and is no longer written). Observing commands run with `gating: false` and never decide the verdict (`reviewVerdict`). One review session judges every queued check and returns `{items:[{id, verdict, detail, file?, line?, quote?}]}`; a `fail` without a file in the diff, an existing repository file or a quote becomes `na` with reason `no evidence` (B1, `hasEvidence`, paths kept inside the workspace). From round 2 the session reads `git diff <previous sha>` and the previous results; a check that passed before and gets no verdict keeps its pass. Model: the Review model, else the summary models, never the project's agent model. `review_finished.results` carry `kind` (and `gating: false` for observing commands, so Insights can tell the verdict).
- Disputes (§6.1, B7): `flowly_update_status(in_review)` takes `disputes: [{id, reason}]` (ids of the latest review's failed items or ones already disputed, idempotent, at most 10, else `INVALID_INPUT`; only with a delivery), stored in `task.disputes` without a task revision; the next review records them as `disputed` with the reason and does not judge them.
- Signals and retirement at review end (§6.6, §6.7, `GuardrailsService.reviewed`): reviews/passes per command and guideline, caught (failed in the card's previous review, passed now, not disputed), `unavailableRun` and observation counts for commands; then `retirement()` over the project's entries with `guardrail_retired` events for pauses and archives (an observing command that earned it becomes active silently). Counts do not change an entry's revision; state changes do. A cancelled review counts nothing.
- Rework message (§6.5): guidelines and items in the user's words, each failed line with `[id: …]` for disputes, the acceptance-list and `disputes=[{id, reason}]` sentence.
- Delivery gate and workpad (§6.1, §7.1): `WORKPAD_OPEN` and `WORKPAD_FAILING` are gone; `acknowledgeOpen` only answers `SETUP_INCOMPLETE`, whose `workpad` item now means "no acceptance list". `Workpad` holds `acceptance`, `revision` and who/when (old `validation`, `confusions`, `env`, `head`, `delivery` are dropped when read); `flowly_update_workpad` and CLI `workpad` take only `acceptance` (anything else is `WORKPAD_INVALID`); `workpads.check` only ticks acceptance; `workpads.resolve-confusion` and `workpads.refresh` removed with the HEAD refresh. Plan snapshots unchanged. Card signal is `Acceptance n/m`.
- Settings and removals (§7.5, §8): `project.autoReview` is `{enabled, maxRounds}` (stored `rules`/`checks`/`goal`/`guidance` kept on disk for the one-time migration and ignored; the next save drops them), `projects.update` accepts only those two; `project.reviewDraft` and `task.reviewSetup` dropped. Removed: `flowly_propose_review_rules` (MCP, CLI `review-rules`, receipts kept readable), `review_rules_proposed`, RPCs `reviews.detect-commands|analyze-history|analysis-status|apply-draft|discard-draft|setup-card`, `insights.add-review-check|pause-review-check|dismiss-review-nudge`, `server/review-history.ts`, `shared/review-history.ts`, `review_rule`/`review_pause` findings (stored ones dropped when read) and `reviewNudges`.
- Weekly run (§4.5, §7.3), inside the Insights run before the model call: with Auto review on, commands whose package script or Makefile target is gone pause (`script missing`) and whitelisted checks (`test*`, `lint*`, `typecheck`, `type-check`, `tsc`, `check`, `vet`) that agents ran on ≥ 3 cards in 30 days (command log keys, any package manager's spelling) join as observing; retirement over every entry; with card summaries off, one Summary-model call in the background workspace over up to 30 `feedback` clues of the last 7 days learns through `GuardrailsService.learn` (`learnedUntil` per project avoids learning twice). The model reads a `# Guardrails` section (guidelines with scope, counts, source cards and "caught 5+ in 30 days", notes, open items, the last 30 days' review results) and may return `merges` (≤ 5, both ids active in the project and of one kind; drop archived `merged` with `supersededBy`, counts summed, sources merged, scope union, `guardrail_retired` event). Anything else the model invents (a guideline, a command, a `review_rule`) is dropped.
- Agent surface (§5.2, §5.3, §9): `get_task` has no `reviewRules`; `review` lists items with kind, gating and dispute reason; `workpad` is `{revision, acceptance}`. `flowly_recall` also returns matching active guidelines (`guidelines`). Skill, turn checklist and tool texts per §9; CLI `status in_review --disputes '<JSON>'`.
- Telemetry (§10): `f_auto_review_llm` removed; `c_guardrails` (active guidelines across unarchived projects, bucketed like `projects`); TELEMETRY-SPEC §4 updated, `c_memory_confirmed` wording now says newly active notes.
- For phase C: `reviewProgress(review)` in `shared/auto-review.ts` (§7.1.1) and `insights.guardrails` (`guardrailsLedger` and `guardrailsLedgerSchema` in `shared/guardrails.ts`). Client edits made only to keep typecheck green are listed in the phase B report; `client/phase-b-orphans.ts` (never imported) keeps the catalog test green for the strings whose UI phase B removed, for phase C to delete with those catalog entries.
- Not done here: Insights "a new command found past the cap" line (§3.4, no channel yet), review `tokens` (the SDK result gives none), the misfire count for disputed goal/card items (no entry to count on).

## Guardrails phase A: foundation (card 2458cff4) — 2026-10-28

- [GUARDRAILS-SPEC](GUARDRAILS-SPEC.md) §3: `guardrails.json` (`shared/guardrails.ts` schemas, limits and pure logic; `server/guardrails.ts` store with serial writes, `.bak`, atomic replace, invalid file kept). One-time migration at daemon start from `memory.json` and every project's `autoReview.rules` (§3.6), queued ahead of all reads; `memory.json` becomes `memory.json.migrated`; a failed migration leaves both files and runs empty. The board's `autoReview.rules` are left in place for phase B to drop.
- Learning (§4.1–§4.3, §4.6): rework turn = the turn opened by the user's own words while the card was In review (`MemoryService.reworkQuote`, the feedback-clue fact). The card summary of such a turn gets the `Guardrails` section and the `correction` field (`shared/summary.ts`, `server/summaries.ts`); `GuardrailsService.learn` keeps card items, promotes the second card into a guideline, counts misses with scope widening, revives paused/archived guidelines, ends a dispute as caught; secrets are recorded as an error, not kept. Events `guardrail_learned` / `guardrail_retired` carry `guardrail: {change, kind, id}` into the flow log.
- Notes (§4.4): `flowly_remember` and the Done retro write active notes at once, `supersedes` archives at once; the `memory.*` RPCs are aliases over the notes until the Guardrails page.
- Commands (§4.5 first bullet): `projects.create` / `create-linked` and switching Auto review on add the detected commands as `observing`; new projects get `autoReview: { enabled: true, maxRounds: 2 }`.
- Prevention (§5.1, §5.2): session instructions carry the guidelines block (in scope by type) before the notes block; `flowly_get_task.guardrails`; `memory.candidates` removed. RPCs `guardrails.read|counts|create|update|delete|detect|retro`; finishing a card closes its items and turns open disputes into misfires.
- Additive model fields for phases B/C: `Task.disputes`, `Review.shape`, `Review.items`, event `guardrail` and `results[].kind`.
- Review rework (orchestrator review of bffa7e5, spec 7964f06): card items stay open after Done/Cancelled; the rework quote has no 15-character minimum; `missed` only when the card's latest `review.items` passed or n/a'd the guideline; a match on a merged or superseded guideline resolves along `supersededBy` (`liveEntry`).
- At the phase B/C merge: the `memory.*` RPCs (read, counts, create, update, confirm, dismiss, restore, archive, archive-many, delete, retro) and `MemoryService`'s human-edit methods (`view`, `counts`, `create`, `update`, `confirm`, `dismiss`, `restore`, `archive`, `remove`, `archiveMany`) were removed, with `memoryViewSchema` and `recallTotals`; the Guardrails page uses `guardrails.*`, and the note edit tests now run on `GuardrailsService`.
- Not in phase A: the review itself (shape, items, evidence filter, disputes input, counters on review end, retirement runs), weekly re-detection and consolidation, `flowly_recall` over guidelines (§5.3), client, i18n, docs other than this entry.

## Telemetry error fixes (card "Telemetry error analysis") — 2026-10-27

- First two days of `$exception` reports (34 events, 35 installs) grouped and mapped to source with `scripts/map-bundle-lines.mjs`: [verification/telemetry-errors-2026-10-27.md](verification/telemetry-errors-2026-10-27.md).
- Local daemon connection (`server/local-daemon.ts`, 13 events): `paseo.pid` addresses are resolved like the `paseo` CLI, including Unix sockets and Windows named pipes (through the bundled `ws`, now a pinned dependency; typed by `server/ws.d.ts` because `@types/ws` breaks the daemon's timer types). An empty address (Paseo restarting the daemon) is `LocalDaemonUnavailable`. Fixes read-state sync, workspace restore, background-workspace reuse, attention clearing, summarizer delete and `paseo_version` on those installs. [WORKSPACE-RESTORE-SPEC](WORKSPACE-RESTORE-SPEC.md)
- Windows CLI (`server/paseo-cli.ts`, 3 events): `paseo.cmd` is started as the command it wraps (`Paseo.exe` + runner + CLI entry, or Node + npm's `bin/paseo`), never through cmd.exe; session settings and native project create/delete share it. [UI-REFRESH-SPEC](UI-REFRESH-SPEC.md)
- Not reported any more (logged as before): `DaemonConnectionError`, `LocalDaemonUnavailable`, `SummaryModelError`. A missing folder or session image is answered with a plain message instead of `ENOENT`. [TELEMETRY-SPEC §11.2](TELEMETRY-SPEC.md)

## Loading R2: no whole-board reads — 2026-10-26

- User decision 2026-10-26: every layout reads the overview plus card detail; no switch back to the whole board and no user option. The maintainer uses it first and fixes what shows up. [Spec](LOADING-R2-SPEC.md)
- Client no longer calls `board.read-changed`: the wide board polls the overview, the drawer reads card detail, Ideas read the project's ideas, the task form reads the project card index (new `externals`), the connection editor reads `linkedCards` from `connectors.connections`, the Next moves and Ideas actions read only their card or project, idea links carry `projectId`. Server handler kept for older clients.
- Parity on a copy of the maintainer's data (107 cards, 4552 events, 118 sessions in 5 attention states each, finished-card cutoffs from 6 h to 30 days): overview facts equal the old whole-board derivations (`docs/verification/loading-r2-2026-10-26/parity.test.ts`). It catches 3 of 4 injected server bugs; the 4th (`hadTurnEnd`) is invisible on this data because every session has a turn end.
- Timing, wide 1700×1000, Flowly project, 3 runs each: first card 577/605/605 ms before, 503/968/463 ms after; first 10 s received 5.3–5.5 MB before, 2.3–2.9 MB after (the rest is Paseo's and Flowly's other reads). `timing-before.json`, `timing-after.json`.
- Live check after installing 5d8cb95 (merged with main after Insights v3): no `board.read`/`board.read-changed` sent while opening the board, a card, a session preview and the New task form; the overview polls every 3 s; a wide session preview keeps polling its card (4 detail reads in 12 s). Screenshots in `ui/` (desktop board, card, session preview, New task; compact board). Phone timing (§4.3) not run.
- `docs/promo/shot-lib.mjs` `hidePrivate` hid the whole compact board (the walk up from the project label reached the column holding the board); it now hides only siblings on the tab's own row.
- Independent verification (Haiku 5.5): first round on 16a117d found the wide preview polling stop, the lost idea-link retry and swallowed read errors (fixed in 24e417c) and parity gaps (closed in 24e417c and 96b4620); re-verification on 24e417c confirmed the fixes. Report: `docs/verification/loading-r2-2026-10-26/README.md`.

## Board data in SQLite (minimal) — 2026-10-26

- User decision 2026-10-26: finish the SQLite move first, loading later; keep rollback and no data loss, otherwise as simple as possible. Replaces the 10-25 full design (D1–D4); the old fixture kernel, specs and evidence are on the local branch `archive/sqlite-r1-v1`. [Spec](SQLITE-STORAGE-SPEC.md).
- `BoardStore` keeps every business rule; only load/save moved behind a persistence seam (`server/board-file.ts` JSON, `server/sqlite-storage.ts` SQLite). One generic `items` table keyed by collection and element key, row-level diff written in one `BEGIN IMMEDIATE`; cached board, owner lock (`storage-owner.sqlite`).
- First start: snapshot to `migrations/`, build `flowly.sqlite.tmp`, integrity check and value-for-value comparison with the existing reader, one rename, then `board.json` becomes a marker older versions refuse. Any failure before the rename stays on JSON and retries at the next start. Daily backups (7), restore with a revision offset, export back to v5 JSON (`storage-mode.json`), Settings → Storage with five RPCs. Attached JSON files, composer, flow logs and read paths unchanged.
- Self-checks: typecheck PASS; `npm test` 129 files / 1056 tests PASS (1 skipped: the crash child); `server/sqlite-store.test.ts` 31, `server/sqlite-parity.test.ts` 1 (the same operations on JSON and SQLite give the same board after every step, project create/remove included), `server/sqlite-crash.test.ts` 3 (real SIGKILL before/after the rename and inside a write). Five deliberate bugs in the row diff and restore were each caught. The agent suites with the fixture on SQLite pass 119/125; the six others build a second JSON store on the same folder or corrupt board.json directly.
- First independent verification (Haiku 5.5, on 2334d58): checks PASS; a real-runtime rehearsal on a copy of the maintainer's data (106 cards, 4515 events, 2730 receipts; 4.9 MB board.json → 7.0 MB database) passed 23/23: move + first read 376 ms, later reads 7 ms, an edit 22 ms. It found an export stopped between its two file writes blocking the next start (fixed: mode file first, a marker means the export did not happen), a failed rename after export leaving every read busy (fixed), same-second backups pruned in the wrong order, a restore that reported success while the database could not open, the migration rename outside its fallback, and 0644 backups/snapshots (all fixed, with tests). Re-verification on 62ec9e9: all PASS, rehearsal 23/23 again (move + first read 488 ms, reads 7 ms); its one Low (a restore that cannot open the backup now says the backup is in place) is fixed with a test. UNRUN: a kill inside the Helper runtime, and the Settings → Storage UI, which needs the plugin installed on real data (the switch).
- Switched the maintainer's data after the merge (user's go-ahead): 365 ms for 107 cards / 4552 events, the backup taken at the move equals the original board.json field for field, reads and writes work, Settings → Storage checked on desktop (compact only partly capturable with shot-lib). See `docs/verification/sqlite-min-2026-10-26/switch.md`.
- Onboarding: tour More features gets Backups; What's new 1.7.0 gets one item with Open settings → Storage. No tip: nothing to point at during normal use.
- Independent milestone verification (claude/claude-haiku-5-5) and the real-data copy rehearsal: see `docs/verification/sqlite-min-2026-10-26/`.

## Native session pane: faster Next moves card, one card style — 2026-10-21

- [x] Requests: (1) on phones the Progress card is quick but the Next moves card is slow; (2) the two timeline cards look different. Spec: NATIVE-SESSION-SPEC §8.1, §5.3, §6.3; SESSION-CHIP-SPEC 2026-10-21 amendment.
- [x] Cause measured on this host (1251 agents, 386 summarizer leftovers): a chip refresh round listed all live agents (233 KB), then every agent including archived (3.1 MB) after any board change, before it computed the Next moves state; rounds ran every 5 s; the daemon wrote the card behind the model lookup (up to 2 s) and a board write. Luna's partial baseline (round 5, CPU rate 4 and 1, raw data in `docs/verification/fixtures/native-session/perf/`, not committed or reported): Progress card visible 13-64 ms after its row, Next moves card 0.4-5.9 s after its row.
- [x] Fix (commit "Next moves card no longer waits for the archived scan"): state first, archived scan after and not on board changes; live sessions from the agent stream; pushed status / user-message / permission changes trigger a round within ~150 ms; a row that arrives first asks for a round; managed card written first in the hook; unchanged chip state wakes no subscriber; timing log entries `chips.round`, `chips.archived`. Unit tests: `client/live-agents.test.ts`, `client/session-chips.test.ts`.
- [x] Style: first tried one frame and header for both cards (commit "Progress and Next steps cards share one frame and header": title and Open card inside the card). The user preferred the original Progress layout (2026-10-21), so it was restored and Next steps aligned to it (commit "Progress card back to its original layout; Next steps aligned to it"): small heading above on both, Open card back in the Progress footer, Next steps' hide button at the right end of its heading; then the user also dropped Next steps' grey card, so its chips sit directly under the heading (`client/native-card.tsx`; `ContentCard` unchanged apart from exporting its frame style). Known and accepted by the user: on a phone the Progress footer is crowded (the timestamp can wrap, the button can be cut to "Open ca…"), as before. Screenshots: `docs/verification/native-session/style/`.
- [x] PASS: `npm run typecheck`; `npm test` 786 passed / 65 skipped (branch), 821 passed on the combined main tree; plugin reloaded, headless load has no page errors; Progress card checked on desktop and a 430 px phone layout.
- [ ] UNRUN (user cancelled the Luna measurement, 2026-10-21: "if there is a performance problem I will come back"): the after-fix Next moves latency, live on a phone or CPU-throttled; the Next moves card with the new style was not screenshotted (no ready card existed at deploy time).

- [x] Request (2026-10-21): after a new turn starts, the previous turn's Next steps disappeared but its space stayed. Measured on the live host: the empty row keeps the host's stream-item wrapper (`marginBottom` 16 px = `SPACING[4]`, `getGapBetweenStreamItems`); a plugin row cannot be removed, a renderer cannot change its wrapper, and a negative child margin is clamped (checked in Chromium). User decision: keep the previous suggestions visible but not pressable. The row is rewritten as `{status:"past", eventId, moves}` (chips disabled, none emphasized, no ×); the user's × still hides a set for good; rows written `dismissed` before this stay empty until the daemon restarts. Cost to know: every earlier turn now leaves its chips in the history. Spec §6.1–6.2; tests in `server/next-move-rows.test.ts`, `server/session-insights.test.ts`, `shared/native-session.test.ts`, `client/session-chips.test.ts`. Onboarding: no tour or tip change (a refinement of an existing card).

## Next moves in the delivery call — 2026-10-20

- [x] Card request: let the calls an agent already makes at the end of a turn carry the next moves, so suggesting them is part of delivering and not a second call that gets dropped. [Spec](NEXT-MOVES-SPEC.md) §8 (reconciled with the concurrent delivery-reminders design §9, which lands on its own card).
- [x] Implemented: `flowly_update_status` and `flowly_report_progress` (all kinds) take an optional `nextMoves` with the `flowly_suggest_next_moves` items; absent records nothing, `[]` records an explicit none (`next_moves_suggested`, `moves: []`, note "No next steps needed"), 1–3 record the suggestion. One transaction writes the status/report event, then the suggestion; one receipt; a refused call (WORKPAD_OPEN, REVISION_CONFLICT, guard) writes nothing; replay does not add a second suggestion. The standalone tool now accepts `[]` too. A `moveTo:"done"` move is only accepted on a call that leaves the card in In review (INVALID_INPUT otherwise; the standalone tool is unchanged). CLI `status` / `note` take `--moves '<JSON>'` or `--no-moves`. Activity shows only the explicit none, as "<agent> suggested no next moves". Tool descriptions and both skill copies say to pass `nextMoves` when delivering, asking or reporting, and keep `flowly_suggest_next_moves` for mid-turn updates. README (both), 1 string in all 9 catalogs. No tour entry or tip: the only visible change is one Activity line.
- [x] PASS: `npm run typecheck`; full `npm test` 93 files / 705 passed, 65 skipped, three runs in a row. New tests: agent-service (delivery with moves in order, every report kind, `[]` replaces and absent keeps, invalid moves write nothing, Done guard incl. finished cards, replay/reuse, WORKPAD_OPEN refusal then resend), next-moves (`isNoMoves`, empty after non-empty), progress-rules (a report with its suggestion still counts as work), tool-server (schemas allow `[]`, descriptions and skill text, CLI `--moves`/`--no-moves` and bad flag combinations, MCP calls). The CLI flow test got a 30 s timeout: it runs a long sequence of real CLI processes and passed 3.2 s alone but timed out at 5 s in one parallel full run after this card added calls to it.
- [x] Checked: Agent habits (`shared/agent-habits.ts`, `shared/insights.ts`) already reads `moves: []` as an explicit none, so the new events count there without a change.
- [x] PASS (live, desktop): after `paseo plugin reload flowly`, two `codex/gpt-6-luna` full-access sessions on a temporary card in the Tinylink fixture (one with the MCP server, one CLI only). `update_status in_review` with `nextMoves` wrote the status change and the suggestion in the same millisecond and showed the chips (3 over MCP, 2 over the CLI); `report_progress` with `[]` and `note --no-moves` wrote `moves: []`, removed the chips and added the "suggested no next moves" Activity row (2 rows); the Done guard refused `moveTo: done` on a non-delivery call with `INVALID_INPUT` and wrote nothing. The card, its 2 sessions and its workspace were deleted and archived afterwards; Tinylink's own cards, workspaces and sessions are unchanged. Evidence: [verification/next-moves-delivery.md](verification/next-moves-delivery.md).

  - The model sees existing suggestions with a short id. It marks a repeat with `updates`, which refreshes the existing suggestion (evidence, cards, `seen` + 1) instead of adding one. It lists duplicates among open suggestions in `merges`.
  - A daemon fallback treats a matching fingerprint, or a similar title (word and CJK-bigram Jaccard: ≥ 0.6 with the same target, ≥ 0.7 with any target), as a repeat.
  - Quiet rule: under 50 new actions, or under 2 cards unless there are at least 200 new actions.
  - The monthly estimate sums the latest run cost of each included project.
  - Insights shows "Seen in N runs".
  - The existing repeats are merged by the next model run. The data file is not edited directly.

## Composer focus during a running session — 2026-10-16

- [x] Card report "On PC, typing in a running session gets interrupted, it happens when content updates, and after the interruption typing is impossible". Cause: the transcript ScrollView had `keyboardDismissMode="on-drag"` on web; RN Web's `_handleScroll` then blurs the focused input on every scroll event, and follow-tail scrolls on each output update. Web now uses `none` ([spec](SESSION-INPUT-SPEC.md)).
- [x] PASS: typecheck; headless app.paseo.sh against the local daemon, typing into this card's running session with forced transcript scrolls: with the fix the textarea kept focus (0 blurs); control run with the old value lost focus to `<body>`, blur stack `_handleScroll → blurTextInput`.
- [ ] UNRUN: the Electron desktop window.

## Workflow stages: In progress → Design / Development / Test — 2026-10-15

- [x] User decisions: split In progress into Design → Development → Test for every project (option B); the agent decides whether to start with Design or Development. Wrote [workflow stages spec](WORKFLOW-STAGES-SPEC.md) and the user approved it ("OK, let's try it, and change it back if it doesn't work").
- [x] Board v4 (`server/migrate.ts`): In progress cards → Development; a configured In progress lane expands in place (on/off kept, custom name → Development); events/receipts keep the retired status (`historyStatusSchema`), shown as "In progress"; one-time `board.pre-v4.json` copy.
- [x] Rules: A1 no longer moves Backlog/Todo; resuming from In review/Blocked returns to the latest enabled work stage (else the first enabled one, else no move). A1r and the silent-turn marker cover all three stages.
- [x] Agents: `allowedStatuses` uses design/development/test/blocked/in_review; `in_progress` is accepted as a deprecated alias (keeps the current stage, else Development) so sessions with the old tool schema still work. Skill, tool description, CLI usage and UI copy updated; status icons CircleEllipsis / CircleDot / CircleGauge (same Circle family as the other statuses; Pencil/ShieldCheck looked a different size).
- [x] PASS: typecheck; `npm test` 231 passed / 63 skipped (new: v3→v4 migration, rejected v4 in_progress, A1 resume/skip/disabled lanes, silent turn per stage, alias). Reloaded the plugin. Live board migrated to v4: 2 In progress cards → Development, Ultra Kanban lanes expanded, 37 history events still read `in_progress`, `board.pre-v4.json` holds the v3 data. A live `flowly_update_status(in_progress)` call from this session (old schema) kept the card in Development.
- [x] Fix (user report): dragging a card to another lane under a non-Manual sort showed "Sorted by priority…" instead of moving it. Cause: web `onLayout` uses ResizeObserver and only reports size changes, so lanes shifted by a sibling collapsing/expanding (or lanes added/hidden) kept a stale x and the drop resolved to the card's own lane. Lanes are now re-measured with `measureInWindow` at drag start; the toast now says lane moves still work. Cross-lane drops under priority/other sorts land by the sort rule.
- [x] Fix (user report, session `fdfe6286`): a rework-of-implementation follow-up resumed the card in Design. The agent had set Design, implemented, and delivered without ever setting Development, so A1 returned to that stale stage. Skill and tool description now require keeping the stage current (Development as soon as code changes; never deliver implementation from Design) and checking the auto-resumed stage at the start of every turn. A1 itself unchanged.
- [x] Follow-up (user: the same card went back to Design again; its session still had the old guidance): A1 now resumes a card **in review in Development** (review feedback is rework; falls back like Blocked when Development is off); Blocked still returns to the previous stage. Skill, settings copy and specs updated; tests cover both paths and the fallback.

## F4 desktop keyboard shortcuts — 2026-10-15

- [x] Researched Paseo 0.11.2 before implementation. The plugin SDK has no shortcut API, and Command Center items have no key field. The host shortcut registry is internal, and a global DOM listener is forbidden. The trade-off is recorded in the [shortcuts spec](SHORTCUTS-SPEC.md), written before code.
- [x] Board-scoped keys on desktop web: N new task, P projects, / or F filters, A attention, ? help, Esc (session Back, then close the sidebar), and J/K/H/L card navigation. The root `View` is focusable and receives the RN-Web `onKeyDown`. Plugin text inputs and all plugin modals block shortcuts through a ref-counted guard (`useShortcutBlock`, `client/modal.tsx`). Modifier chords, repeats and IME input pass through.
- [x] Discoverability: a desktop header Keyboard button opens a help modal; header button labels include their key. Five global Command Center items open the board with a once-only, 30-second `intent` param.

## 2026-10-21 External items can be registered as outputs (card "External items (PR/MR etc.) can also be registered as outputs")

- An agent's own pull/merge request can now be registered with `role=output`
  ([CONNECTORS-SPEC](CONNECTORS-SPEC.md) §3.1, [CARD-ARTIFACTS-SPEC](CARD-ARTIFACTS-SPEC.md) §2): the server no longer
  forces external items to `input`, and editing can change their role. Paste and the + menu picker still default to
  input.
- An agent re-adding an entry the user added with another role gets a `notice` in the tool result (kept in the receipt,
  so a replay returns it) instead of a silent no-op.
- Output items keep their live status; their unread follows new comments and reviews only (fingerprint
  `<content>~<discussion>`), so the agent's own pushes do not mark its PR unread. Inputs compare as before.
- Skill (both copies): "part of the task's requirements" is limited to inputs; own PR/MR goes in as output.
- Existing artifacts forced to input before this change are not migrated.
- Onboarding: bug fix of an agent-facing rule; no welcome-tour or one-time tip change.
